CHILD Syndrome

Mendelian MONDO:0010621 Pathograph 28 Show in embeddings browser sterol biosynthesis disorder X-linked ichthyosis syndrome

CHILD syndrome (congenital hemidysplasia with ichthyosiform nevus and limb defects) is an X-linked, usually male-lethal disorder caused by pathogenic NSDHL variants that impair post-squalene cholesterol biosynthesis. Heterozygous females have variable cutaneous and skeletal involvement, typically with unilateral ichthyosiform lesions and ipsilateral limb defects; bilateral or mild presentations and rare mosaic males are reported. Intellect is usually normal. NSDHL participates in sterol C4-demethylation, and experimental models distinguish effects of reduced cholesterol availability from effects of accumulated intermediates. Patient skin shows abnormal epidermal lipid secretion. X-inactivation creates mosaic expression, but the mechanism of the characteristic body-half distribution and any contribution of longitudinal clone depletion in human skin remain unresolved. Topical statins, alone or combined with cholesterol, have improved skin lesions in uncontrolled reports; these observations do not prove that cholesterol supplementation is required or that congenital skeletal defects are corrected. Hypomorphic NSDHL variants cause the distinct allelic disorder CK syndrome.

Inheritance

2
X-linked dominant inheritance with male lethality HP:0001423
CHILD syndrome is inherited in an X-linked manner and predominantly affects heterozygous females because hemizygous males with severe loss-of-function variants usually do not survive gestation. Rare surviving males with postzygotic mosaicism have been reported. A heterozygous mother has a 50% chance of transmitting the pathogenic variant in each pregnancy; the expected live-born distribution is altered by male lethality. Molecular recurrence-risk assessment requires a pathogenic or likely pathogenic variant, not a variant of uncertain significance.
X-linked dominant inheritance
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:10710235 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We report for the first time that CHILD syndrome (MIM 308050), an X-linked dominant, male-lethal trait characterized by an inflammatory nevus with striking lateralization and strict midline demarcation, as well as ipsilateral hypoplasia of the body is caused by mutations in the gene NSDHL located at Xq28"
The gene-discovery paper states the X-linked dominant, male-lethal mode of inheritance alongside the causal locus.
PMID:21290788 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"CHILD syndrome is usually male lethal during gestation and thus predominantly affects females."
GeneReviews states the male-lethal consequence and the resulting female predominance.
PMID:21290788 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"However, since studies suggest that male conceptuses with an NSDHL pathogenic variant generally abort or resorb spontaneously, the expected live-born distribution is: 33% heterozygous (typically) affected females; 33% unaffected females; and 33% unaffected males."
Supplies the quantitative recurrence-risk figures that this description would otherwise only paraphrase, including the reason the live-born ratios depart from the usual X-linked expectation.
+ 1 more reference
Functional mosaicism from random X inactivation
Heterozygous females are functional mosaics because of X-inactivation; this is distinct from a postzygotic DNA variant. In cultures from one studied patient, both NSDHL alleles were present in genomic DNA from affected and unaffected skin. Affected keratinocytes expressed the mutant allele, whereas unaffected keratinocytes expressed the wild-type allele. Affected fibroblasts retained expression of both alleles. This tissue-specific expression pattern does not establish the developmental origin of the body-half boundary or longitudinal clearance of mutant cells in human skin.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:21753784 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"However, RT-PCR on cDNA from keratinocytes and fibroblasts isolated from the affected versus clinically unaffected side in Patient #1 demonstrated exclusive expression of the mutant allele by affected cultured keratinocytes, while unaffected keratinocytes solely expressed the wild type allele"
Cultures from one patient showed side-specific NSDHL allele expression; this is not a longitudinal measure of cell clearance.
PMID:21753784 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Only the normal allele was expressed by unaffected fibroblasts, but fibroblasts from the clinically affected area expressed an approximately even distribution of normal and mutant alleles."
Fibroblast expression in affected skin did not match the exclusively mutant keratinocyte pattern.
PMID:11907515 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Apparently, the effect of random X-inactivation is responsible for different patterns of cutaneous involvement in female carriers of NSDHL mutations."
Attributes the variable cutaneous distribution, including the exceptional bilateral case, to the X-inactivation pattern rather than to allele identity.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

3
What mechanism converts random X inactivation into a body-half boundary in CHILD syndrome, rather than the finer Blaschko-line mosaicism seen in other X-linked genodermatoses?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lateralization_mechanism_unknown
Attached to
pathophysiology#X-Inactivation-Dependent NSDHL Expression Mosaicism
Expression differences between cultured keratinocytes from affected and unaffected patient skin are consistent with functional mosaicism but do not explain the geometry of the boundary. Age-dependent selection in mouse liver and brain is a separate observation; human skin clone clearance has not been measured longitudinally. Mouse and canine lesion patterns differ from typical human lateralization. Paired spatial and allele-expression measurements could distinguish developmental lineage allocation from subsequent tissue-specific selection.
Proposed experiments
Lineage and allele-expression mapping across the midline in CHILD skin
lateralization_lineage_mapping
Single-cell or spatial transcriptomic mapping of NSDHL allele expression across the midline boundary in affected individuals, paired with clonal lineage inference, to determine whether the boundary reflects an early embryonic left-right lineage restriction, a later selective clearance front, or an unrecognized signalling boundary.
Show evidence (3 references)

Cultures from one patient showed side-specific NSDHL allele expression; this is not a longitudinal measure of cell clearance.
PMID:21753784 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Only the normal allele was expressed by unaffected fibroblasts, but fibroblasts from the clinically affected area expressed an approximately even distribution of normal and mutant alleles."
Fibroblast expression in affected skin did not match the exclusively mutant keratinocyte pattern.
PMID:19631568 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Model Organism
"These data suggest that the higher percentage of WT hepatocytes present in livers from older Bpa1H females is the cumulative result of postnatal differences in either the growth rate or survival of WT vs mutant hepatocytes."
Cross-sectional age groups support selection in mouse liver while leaving differential survival versus proliferation unresolved.
Do the placental abnormalities associated with prenatal death in Nsdhl-mutant mice contribute to human male lethality?
HUMAN MODEL MISMATCH OPEN male_lethality_placental_mechanism_human
Attached to
pathophysiology#Placental Labyrinth Dysmorphogenesis
Nsdhl mouse studies associate placental labyrinth abnormalities with prenatal death, but the timing varies by allele and the findings do not establish a universal human mechanism. Rodent and human placental X-inactivation differ. A report of recurrent male pregnancy losses identified maternal and fetal NSDHL c.790-6C>T, but classified it as a variant of uncertain significance, predicted a splice effect without functional confirmation, and tested only one lost conceptus. That report does not confirm NSDHL-mediated recurrent fetal lethality or the placental mechanism in humans.
Proposed experiments
NSDHL-null human trophoblast organoid vascularization assay
human_nsdhl_trophoblast_model
Generate NSDHL loss-of-function human trophoblast stem cell or placental organoid models and assay Hedgehog pathway activity, trophoblast proliferation, and vascular network formation, to test whether the murine labyrinth phenotype has a human counterpart independent of rodent-specific placental X inactivation.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:15639195 SUPPORT Model Organism
"X-inactivation is non-random in females in most lineages of the rodent placenta with preferential inactivation of the paternal X chromosome."
States the rodent-specific placental X-inactivation arrangement that the mouse inference depends on and that does not transfer to humans.
PMID:36504312 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"According to ACMG guidelines, the variant has been classified as a variant of uncertain significance."
The primary report explicitly classifies c.790-6C>T as uncertain; its more assertive abstract does not establish causality.
PMID:36504312 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"However, further work is required to verify the relationship between the c.790-6C>T variant and CHILD syndrome."
The authors acknowledge that the variant-disease relationship requires further verification.
How should the historical EBP-positive case labeled CHILD syndrome be interpreted within the current NSDHL-defined disease boundary?
INTERPRETATION OPEN nsdhl_versus_ebp_nosological_boundary
Attached to
genetic#NSDHL
An early report identified an EBP nonsense variant and a CDPX2 plasma sterol profile in a patient clinically labeled CHILD syndrome. The NSDHL discovery cohort instead found EBP unaffected, and current GeneReviews defines CHILD syndrome through NSDHL while listing EBP-related CDPX2 as a differential diagnosis. The early clinical label alone is insufficient evidence of locus heterogeneity. Reassessment of historical cases may clarify phenotypic overlap, but does not justify recording EBP as causative for NSDHL-related CHILD syndrome.
Proposed experiments
Genotype and sterol reassessment of the assembled CHILD case literature
child_locus_reassessment_cohort
Assemble the published CHILD syndrome cases with molecular data, and where material allows, determine NSDHL versus EBP genotype together with plasma sterol profile and lesion distribution, to establish whether EBP-related cases form a clinically separable subgroup or are distributed through the CHILD phenotype.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:10710233 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We speculate that at least some cases of CHILD syndrome are allelic with CDPX2 caused by 3beta-hydroxysteroid-delta8,delta7-isomerase deficiency."
The authors' own hedged phrasing is the open question this discussion records.

Pathophysiology

13
NSDHL Loss of Function
Mechanism confidence: Established
Pathogenic NSDHL variants impair the sterol dehydrogenase/decarboxylase component of the C4-demethylation complex. An expression study of thirteen disease-associated missense variants and one deletion found reduced protein abundance; temperature or glycerol partially increased abundance for some variants. These assays do not establish that all variants act through the same folding defect or that rescued protein has restored catalytic activity.
NSDHL hgnc:13398 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves NSDHL (hgnc:13398). hgnc:13398 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.

GO:0000252 is a molecular function from the Gene Ontology. ⇓ LOSS OF FUNCTION

Impaired Sterol C4-Demethylation Loss of the NSDHL enzyme component impairs this biosynthetic step.
Increased Receptor Lysosomal Trafficking Experimental NSDHL depletion alters receptor handling; the molecular intermediates and relevance to human skin remain incompletely defined.
Impaired Anagen Hair Growth Nsdhl-deficient mouse skin shows defective anagen progression, with the intervening signaling sequence unresolved.
Ipsilateral body hypoplasia NSDHL dysfunction causes the developmental syndrome that includes ipsilateral body hypoplasia. The intervening tissue-specific processes are unresolved; this edge does not assign the phenotype to a demonstrated Hedgehog or clone-clearance pathway.
Ipsilateral limb hypoplasia or aplasia NSDHL dysfunction underlies the limb-reduction phenotype through incompletely resolved developmental processes. A specific Hedgehog-mediated route in human limbs is not established by the mouse neural-culture experiments.

"Here we show that protein expression levels were low for all mutants, but some could be rescued by a lower temperature (30°C vs. 37°C) and/or the chemical chaperone glycerol."
The expression assay reports lower steady-state abundance and partial abundance rescue; enzymatic rescue is not established by this quote.
PMID:10710235 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Other
"(NAD(P)H steroid dehydrogenase-like protein) encoding a 3beta-hydroxysteroid dehydrogenase functioning in the cholesterol biosynthetic pathway"
Identifies the enzymatic function that is lost.
Impaired Sterol C4-Demethylation
Mechanism confidence: Established
Impaired NSDHL activity limits removal of C4 methyl groups from sterol intermediates. Reduced downstream cholesterol supply and upstream precursor accumulation are distinct biochemical consequences.
cholesterol biosynthetic process GO:0006695 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased cholesterol biosynthetic process (GO:0006695). GO:0006695 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ↓ DECREASED

Reduced Cholesterol Availability The biosynthetic defect can reduce cholesterol availability within affected cells.
C4-Methylsterol Accumulation Intermediates upstream of the enzymatic block accumulate.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:15639195 SUPPORT DIRECT BACKGROUND Other
"NSDHL is a 3beta-hydroxysterol dehydrogenase that is involved in the removal of C-4 methyl groups in one of the later steps of cholesterol biosynthesis."
The primary mouse paper describes the established enzymatic function in its background.
PMID:24060582 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"CHILD syndrome results from loss of function mutations in the NSDHL gene, which leads to inhibition of cholesterol synthesis and accumulation of toxic metabolic intermediates in affected tissues."
The review describes reduced synthesis and precursor accumulation; these are separate consequences of the biosynthetic block.
Reduced Cholesterol Availability
Mechanism confidence: Provisional
Cholesterol supply can be insufficient within affected cells despite near-normal circulating sterols. In conditional-null mouse granule-cell cultures, cholesterol or LDL supplementation restored much of the impaired SHH response. This does not imply uniform systemic hypocholesterolemia.
Downstream
Impaired Hedgehog Signal Transduction Cultured granule-cell experiments support impaired reception of SHH when cholesterol is limiting.
Impaired Epidermal Lamellar Body Secretion Reduced availability of a barrier lipid is proposed to contribute to the abnormal secretory apparatus; patient ultrastructure and treatment response do not isolate this contribution from precursor effects.

"Taken together, these results demonstrate that cholesterol depletion is sufficient to account for virtually all of the reduced response of mutant GCPs to SHH in vitro."
Cholesterol supplementation experiments locate a major SHH-response defect in the recipient granule-cell cultures.
C4-Methylsterol Accumulation
Mechanism confidence: Established
C4-methylsterol intermediates accumulate upstream of the NSDHL block. Mouse granule-cell experiments associate precursor reduction with improved viability, but different sterol species and endpoints need separate interpretation. The same intervention did not restore SHH-induced Gli1 expression.
Experimental models
Conditional-null mouse cerebellar granule-cell precursor cultures PRIMARY CELL CULTURE Upstream inhibition reduces precursor accumulation and improves mutant-cell viability without restoring SHH-induced Gli1 by itself.
Downstream
Impaired Epidermal Lamellar Body Secretion Precursor-associated toxicity is a proposed contributor to the secretory abnormality in patient skin; the specific responsible sterols and intermediate steps remain unresolved.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:25652406 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"The concentration of methylsterols was significantly higher in NsdhlΔ5/Y GCPs than Nsdhlflx5/Y GCPs (P < 0.002) in all four culture conditions."
Cultured conditional-null mouse granule-cell precursors accumulate methylsterols under the tested conditions.
PMID:25652406 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Ketoconazole treatment did not affect the viability of WT cells, but it significantly increased mutant cell viability (from 65 to 78%; Fig. 7B)."
An upstream synthesis inhibitor improved viability in mutant cultures; this supports a precursor-associated injury component but does not identify every accumulated sterol as toxic.
PMID:25652406 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Although the reduction of methylsterol levels by ketoconazole treatment apparently improved the viability and cell morphology of the mutant GCPs, it had no effect on Gli1 expression"
Cell viability and SHH transcriptional responses were experimentally dissociable; lowering precursors alone did not restore this readout.
X-Inactivation-Dependent NSDHL Expression Mosaicism
Mechanism confidence: Established
X-inactivation creates functional mosaicism in heterozygous females. In one patient, cultured keratinocytes from affected and unaffected skin expressed different NSDHL alleles, while affected fibroblasts retained both. These observations do not explain the precise midline boundary or demonstrate longitudinal cell clearance.

Age-Dependent Depletion of NSDHL-Deficient Cells
Mechanism confidence: Provisional
Mouse liver and brain show an age-dependent reduction in the representation of NSDHL-deficient cells. This is tissue-specific: NSDHL-negative skin persisted at eight weeks. The mechanism may involve differential survival or proliferation, and the result has not been measured longitudinally in human CHILD skin.
Animal models
Bare patches and striated mice (Nsdhl mutant) Mouse partially recapitulates Cross-sectional mouse age groups show a higher proportion of NSDHL-positive hepatocytes with age.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:19631568 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Model Organism
"These data suggest that the higher percentage of WT hepatocytes present in livers from older Bpa1H females is the cumulative result of postnatal differences in either the growth rate or survival of WT vs mutant hepatocytes."
Cross-sectional age groups support selection in mouse liver while leaving differential survival versus proliferation unresolved.
PMID:19631568 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Model Organism
"The average percentage of NSDHL positive hepatocytes in livers from six Bpa1H P6 females was 52 (SD: 5). Among six mutant livers at P25, the average percentage of NSDHL positive hepatocytes increased to 62 (SD: 9)."
The comparison concerns different mouse age groups and liver tissue, not serial biopsies of human skin.
Impaired Epidermal Lamellar Body Secretion
Mechanism confidence: Established
Patient biopsies show lamellar bodies with few internal lamellae, premature fusion, and incomplete secretion. The resulting retention of organelle material is a cellular secretory defect distinct from the extracellular lipid architecture it produces.

Independent ultrastructural confirmation of the abnormal lamellar granules and of the dermal foam cells this description asserts, from a different patient and laboratory than the treatment study.
Disorganized Stratum Corneum Lipid Lamellae
Mechanism confidence: Established
The stratum corneum extracellular matrix in affected skin contains sparse lamellar material mixed with non-lamellar vesicles, consistent with a defective lipid barrier. Clinically unaffected skin can retain mild ultrastructural abnormalities.

The patient ultrastructure links impaired secretion to disorganized extracellular lipid lamellae.

"Although no visible ichthyosiform features could be appreciated either clinically or by light microscopy, ultrastructural images displayed mild abnormalities in lamellar body internal contents and lamellar bilayer organization"
Clinically unaffected skin from one patient had subtle structural abnormalities, so visible normality is not proof of a fully normal lipid barrier.
Impaired Hedgehog Signal Transduction
Mechanism confidence: Provisional
Conditional Nsdhl-null mouse granule-cell cultures have an impaired response to SHH that is strongly improved by cholesterol supply. Reduced reporter activity is also observed in mouse cerebellum and placenta. These findings concern signal reception and response; defective ligand processing and the precise mechanisms of human limb defects are not established.

Placental Labyrinth Dysmorphogenesis
Mechanism confidence: Provisional
Several Nsdhl mouse alleles show a thin placental labyrinth with reduced fetal vascularization and trophoblast proliferation. Embryonic death occurs at allele-dependent stages, including earlier death with Bpa1H. Placental abnormalities are associated with lethality in these models; the corresponding human mechanism remains unverified.

Several mouse alleles show a placental structural phenotype associated with prenatal lethality; it does not establish the cause of every male loss or a human placental lesion.

Increased Receptor Lysosomal Trafficking
Mechanism confidence: Provisional
NSDHL depletion in experimental cell systems reduces EGFR recycling and shifts receptor-associated signals toward late endosomal and lysosomal compartments. The assays use knockdown and model cells, not direct measurements in human CHILD lesions.
Experimental models
NSDHL-depleted cell models of receptor trafficking CELL LINE NSDHL depletion shifts receptor-associated signals away from recycling endosomes and toward late endosomal compartments.
Downstream
Reduced EGFR Signaling Accelerated receptor degradation can reduce the signaling receptor pool; mouse-skin observations support reduced pathway activation in NSDHL-null regions.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23125191 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"In contrast, depletion of either SC4MOL or NSDHL significantly reduced the amount of fluorescently labeled EGFR ... associated with RAB11-positive recycling endosomes after 30 minutes of EGF stimulation."
NSDHL knockdown in experimental cells reduced receptor localization to recycling endosomes; this is not a patient-tissue result.
PMID:23125191 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Conversely, SC4MOL and NSDHL-deficient cells showed an accelerated concentration of internalized fluorescently labeled EGF in RAB7-positive ... or EGFR in LAMP1-positive late endosomes"
Experimental depletion shifts receptor-associated signals toward late endosomal compartments.
Reduced EGFR Signaling
Mechanism confidence: Provisional
NSDHL-null patches of mouse skin show reduced activated EGFR, ERK, and AKT signals with reduced proliferation and loricrin expression. These model observations suggest altered epidermal growth and differentiation, but do not demonstrate the same signaling state in patient skin.
Animal models
Bare patches and striated mice (Nsdhl mutant) Mouse partially recapitulates NSDHL-null mouse skin has reduced activated EGFR and downstream signals with reduced proliferation.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23125191 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Model Organism
"The reduced intracellular EGFR staining was accompanied by a marked loss of phosphorylated (activated) Y1173-EGFR ... signal in the NSDHL-null areas."
NSDHL-null patches of mouse skin had reduced activated EGFR; the original sentence also reports reduced ERK and AKT phosphorylation.
PMID:23125191 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Model Organism
"The NSDHL-null areas of the skin also exhibited significantly reduced proliferation, as determined by Ki-67 ... expression and in vivo BrdU incorporation"
Mouse skin proliferation was reduced, so human hyperkeratosis must not be generalized into a universal increase in cellular proliferation.
Impaired Anagen Hair Growth
Mechanism confidence: Provisional
Bpa1H mouse skin has impaired postnatal anagen progression despite apparently normal early follicle morphology. In treated CHILD patients, improvement of the skin lesions did not restore hair growth. A specific Hedgehog-mediated explanation for the human hair phenotype remains an inference.
Animal models
Bare patches and striated mice (Nsdhl mutant) Mouse partially recapitulates Mutant mouse skin has impaired anagen growth despite apparently normal early follicle morphology.
Downstream
Alopecia of affected skin Defective follicle growth in Nsdhl mouse skin is consistent with the human failure of hair growth; the intervening human pathway has not been demonstrated.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:19631568 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Model Organism
"The mutant follicles were smaller and shorter than adjacent WT follicles and lacked hair shafts, consistent with defective anagen hair growth."
Mouse mutant skin shows defective postnatal anagen growth; initial P2 follicle morphology was not grossly abnormal.
PMID:21753784 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"By 6 months, treated skin largely normalized, but did not grow hair"
Skin improvement and hair growth were distinct outcomes in two treated patients.
✶

Histopathology

4
Psoriasiform hyperkeratosis and acanthosis with loss of the granular layer
Lesional skin shows marked hyperkeratosis and acanthosis with absence of the granular layer, together with foam cells in the dermis. This is the routine light-microscopic picture, and it reverses on effective pathogenesis-based therapy: after three months of topical cholesterol plus lovastatin the epidermis was largely normal with a prominent granular layer and orthokeratotic scale.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21753784 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Baseline biopsy sections of skin lesions from both patients prior to treatment showed marked hyperkeratosis and acanthosis with absence of the granular layer and dermal foam cells"
Reports the light-microscopic findings in lesional skin from both patients, including the dermal foam cells.
Verruciform xanthoma-like change in the CHILD nevus
Verruciform xanthoma-like histology can occur in the CHILD nevus, together with psoriasiform changes. It is not specific enough to establish the diagnosis by itself. A prominent vulvar and inner-thigh lesion required debulking in one patient in the topical-treatment report.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:7640190 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The associated CHILD nevus shows unique features such as a diffuse form of lateralization, ptychotropism, and microscopic changes of verruciform xanthoma."
The review describes verruciform xanthoma-like changes in CHILD nevi; the finding is not diagnostic by itself.
PMID:16776722 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The histology shared many features with verruciform xanthoma."
Independent case confirmation of the verruciform xanthoma resemblance.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK51754/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK51754.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"this is a nonspecific histopathologic finding that can be seen in other entities but may help distinguish CHILD nevi from other psoriasiform lesions on histopathology."
GeneReviews explicitly limits the specificity of verruciform xanthoma-like histology.
Abnormal lamellar granules on electron microscopy
Ultrastructurally the lesion is a lipid secretory failure. Vesicular structures appear in the intercellular spaces of the stratum corneum and vacuoles in the upper prickle cell layer, some recognizable as abnormal lamellar granules, with large vacuoles inside the foamy cells of the papillary dermis. This is the electron-microscopic counterpart of the lamellar body secretory defect modelled in the pathophysiology, observed in a separate patient and laboratory from the treatment study.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:16776722 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"These findings suggested that abnormal lipid metabolism involving lamellar granules may be responsible to the skin lesion of CHILD syndrome."
States the authors' interpretation linking the ultrastructural finding to the lipid-metabolic lesion, preserving the hedged form they used.
Gastrointestinal mucosal xanthomatous infiltrates
Upper gastrointestinal biopsies in a molecularly confirmed adult showed finely vacuolated xanthoma cells in the lamina propria, positive for the macrophage marker CD163.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:34957706 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"An esophagogastroduodenoscopy revealed multiple whitish plaques in the proximal duodenum, stomach and distal oesophagus ... Biopsies of these areas showed similar xanthoma cells with finely vacuolated cytoplasm in the lamina propria"
A molecularly confirmed adult had gastrointestinal mucosal xanthomas demonstrated by endoscopy and biopsy.
PMID:34957706 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"These cells were positive for CD163 stain ... confirming their macrophage lineage and the diagnosis of gastrointestinal xanthomatosis."
Immunostaining identifies the mucosal xanthoma cells as macrophages.

Genetic Associations

1
NSDHL
Gene: NSDHL hgnc:13398 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is NSDHL (hgnc:13398). hgnc:13398 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:10710235 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"SSCA and genomic sequence analysis of NSDHL identified in 6 patients with CHILD syndrome, including one boy as well as a mother and her daughter, mutations potentially impairing protein function."
Establishes NSDHL variants as the molecular cause in a patient series.
PMID:21290788 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The diagnosis of CHILD syndrome is established in a female proband with a heterozygous NSDHL pathogenic variant identified by molecular genetic testing that results in loss of functional decarboxylating sterol-4-alpha-carboxylate 3-dehydrogenase, the protein encoded by NSDHL."
GeneReviews states the diagnostic criterion and the loss-of-function consequence at the protein level.
PMID:21129721 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"These two mutations, which alter protein folding, show temperature-sensitive protein stability and complementation in Erg26-deficient yeast."
The original CK syndrome study demonstrates residual, temperature-sensitive function of its two studied alleles, supporting the distinction from severe CHILD loss of function.

Medical Actions

8
Topical Cholesterol plus Statin (Pathogenesis-Based Therapy)
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: cholesterol CHEBI:16113 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses cholesterol (CHEBI:16113). CHEBI:16113 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest. lovastatin CHEBI:40303 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses lovastatin (CHEBI:40303). CHEBI:40303 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest. simvastatin CHEBI:9150 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses simvastatin (CHEBI:9150). CHEBI:9150 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Topical lovastatin or simvastatin combined with cholesterol has improved cutaneous lesions in uncontrolled reports. In the original two-patient study, 10% cholesterol alone produced no improvement over three months, followed by substantial improvement with 2% lovastatin/2% cholesterol. There was no statin-only arm, so this sequence does not establish that cholesterol supplementation is required. Later reports describe benefit from statin monotherapy. Skin improvement does not establish correction of congenital skeletal defects, and long-term comparative efficacy and safety remain uncertain.
Mechanism Target:
RESTORES Reduced Cholesterol Availability — The cholesterol component supplies the pathway end product; tissue-specific target engagement was not isolated from the effects of the statin in the clinical report.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21753784 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"On the basis of the putative pathogenic role of both pathway-product deficiency of cholesterol and accumulation of toxic metabolic intermediates, we assessed the efficacy of combined therapy with lovastatin and cholesterol."
The authors state the dual treatment rationale as putative. The combined intervention does not isolate restoration of cholesterol from suppression of precursor formation.
INHIBITS C4-Methylsterol Accumulation — Upstream statin inhibition is intended to reduce precursor production; clinical response to the combination is consistent with this rationale but does not quantify precursor clearance.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21753784 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"On the basis of the putative pathogenic role of both pathway-product deficiency of cholesterol and accumulation of toxic metabolic intermediates, we assessed the efficacy of combined therapy with lovastatin and cholesterol."
The authors state the dual treatment rationale as putative. The combined intervention does not isolate restoration of cholesterol from suppression of precursor formation.
Show evidence (6 references)
PMID:21753784 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Topical treatment with lovastatin/cholesterol (but not cholesterol alone) virtually cleared skin lesions by 3 months, accompanied by histological and ultrastructural normalization of epidermal structure and lipid secretion."
Cholesterol alone did not improve the lesions in this two-patient sequence; there was no statin-only comparison to establish that the combination is required.
PMID:25845514 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We report the case of a 10-year-old girl treated with topical simvastatin and cholesterol ointment, after which her skin lesions significantly improved within the first 30 days of treatment."
Independent case replication using simvastatin in place of lovastatin.
PMID:31365666 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The authors report a 2-month-old patient presenting with typical features of CHILD syndrome that was treated with a topical solution containing cholesterol and lovastatin, with complete clearance of her CHILD nevus."
An additional infant case had clearance of the nevus with the combination.

Topical Simvastatin Monotherapy
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: simvastatin CHEBI:9150 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses simvastatin (CHEBI:9150). CHEBI:9150 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Uncontrolled reports describe improvement with topical simvastatin without added cholesterol. In an infant treated with 2% ointment twice daily, improvement appeared by three weeks and the skin largely normalized by six weeks, with maintenance treatment over ten months. A later 5% report describes variable responses by site and recurrent lesions; it follows the previously reported p.Gly44Ser patient rather than adding an independent genotype case. Comparative efficacy and long-term safety are not established.
Show evidence (6 references)
PMID:29687057 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"After obtaining informed consent from her father, we began treatment with twice-daily topical simvastatin 2% ointment"
The primary case used simvastatin monotherapy.
PMID:29687057 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Decreased inflammation, skin thickening, and scaling were noted as early as 3 weeks. By 6 weeks, the treated skin largely and dramatically normalized with a discrete postinflammatory hypopigmentation remaining"
Clinical results describe improvement by three weeks and near-normalization at six weeks.
PMID:29687057 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"No reoccurrence was observed after 10 months of maintenance topical treatment"
Continued maintenance accompanied the reported sustained response.

Topical Ketoconazole
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: ketoconazole CHEBI:47519 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses ketoconazole (CHEBI:47519). CHEBI:47519 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Off-label topical 2% ketoconazole has improved cutaneous lesions in individual reports. In one adult using it twice daily, itch improved after two days, erythema and scaling improved by day seven, and lesions were substantially improved by day 40. DLQI decreased from 3 to 1, with no apparent adverse effects during two and a half months of follow-up. This uncontrolled observation does not establish equivalence to statins or durable remission after stopping treatment.
Show evidence (5 references)
PMID:39565229 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Institutional approval was obtained for the off-label use of 2% ketoconazole cream. The pre-treatment Dermatology Life Quality Index (DLQI) score was 3 out of 30."
The adult report specifies the off-label formulation and baseline quality-of-life score.
PMID:39565229 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"The itching improved after 2 days of treatment. At 7 days after the start of the treatment, the area of erythema was unchanged, but the desquamation had generally disappeared and the redness of the erythema had improved"
The clinical endpoints distinguish symptom improvement from the unchanged area at one week.
PMID:39565229 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"At 40 days of treatment, the skin lesions had significantly improved, with only minimal pigmentation."
The report describes substantial improvement by day 40.

Symptomatic Topical Care
Action: Supportive CareNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Supportive Care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15747
Platform: Other
Lactic acid preparations can reduce itching and urea creams can help dry skin. Emollients, keratolytics and other topical anti-inflammatory treatments have had variable benefit in individual reports. GeneReviews also describes poorly tolerated or inconsistent responses to oral etretinate. These observations do not establish comparative efficacy.

Skin Grafting from the Unaffected Side
Action: Skin TransplantationNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Skin Transplantation (NCIT:C15325). NCIT:C15325 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15325
Platform: Surgery
Grafting skin from the unaffected contralateral region has been successful in an individual report. GeneReviews also describes recurrence within eight months after dermabrasion; these sparse reports do not establish comparative surgical effectiveness.
Mechanism Target:
BYPASSES X-Inactivation-Dependent NSDHL Expression Mosaicism — Grafting replaces mutant-expressing epidermis with wild-type-expressing epidermis, bypassing rather than correcting the mosaic distribution.

"Treatment of an inflammatory nevus by grafting skin obtained from a contralateral unaffected region has been successful."
Reports the intervention and its dependence on an unaffected-side donor site, which is what makes it a mosaic-bypassing strategy.

Platform: Surgery
Braces and corrective surgery are used for scoliosis and joint contractures. Prosthetic fitting and rehabilitation may be needed for severe limb reduction. In one reported patient, skin improvement permitted use of a prosthesis; this was a functional benefit without reversal of the congenital skeletal defect.

Initial Multisystem Assessment and Surveillance
Action: Supportive CareNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Supportive Care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15747
Platform: Other
GeneReviews recommends initial assessment of cutaneous, skeletal, neurologic, cardiac, pulmonary and renal involvement, including echocardiography, chest imaging and abdominal/pelvic ultrasound. Developmental assessment and neurologic evaluation address CNS manifestations. Subsequent monitoring for skin lesions, scoliosis, contractures and neurologic, cardiac or renal manifestations is annual or as needed, with relevant specialist care. These recommendations reflect the GeneReviews authors' experience rather than a formal clinical practice guideline.

Genetic Counseling
Action: Genetic CounselingNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Genetic Counseling (NCIT:C15240). NCIT:C15240 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15240
Platform: Other
Counseling addresses X-linked inheritance, a 50% transmission probability for a heterozygous mother, variable expression in females and the effect of usual hemizygous male lethality on live-born ratios. Once a familial pathogenic variant is identified, testing of relatives and prenatal or preimplantation testing are possible. A VUS alone does not establish a molecular diagnosis or a variant-specific recurrence risk.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:21290788 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Once the NSDHL pathogenic variant has been identified in a family member with an NSDHL-related disorder, prenatal and preimplantation genetic testing are possible."
States the reproductive testing options that counseling is built around.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK51754/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK51754.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The chance of transmitting the pathogenic variant in each pregnancy is 50%."
GeneReviews states the transmission probability for a heterozygous mother.

Diagnosis

2
NSDHL molecular genetic testing
Diagnosis is supported by characteristic findings and a pathogenic or likely pathogenic NSDHL variant. Sequence analysis detects many coding and splice variants, while exon-level deletion/duplication analysis is needed for copy-number variants that conventional sequencing can miss. A variant of uncertain significance neither establishes nor excludes the diagnosis. Rare mosaic males and mild females require clinical and molecular interpretation rather than exclusion by sex or by absence of a severe limb defect. Optical genome mapping has been reported in a mixed cohort of incontinentia pigmenti and CHILD syndrome; this does not establish a general diagnostic yield for CHILD syndrome.
Genetic Testing NCIT:C15709 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:21290788 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The diagnosis of CHILD syndrome is established in a female proband with a heterozygous NSDHL pathogenic variant identified by molecular genetic testing that results in loss of functional decarboxylating sterol-4-alpha-carboxylate 3-dehydrogenase, the protein encoded by NSDHL."
GeneReviews states the diagnostic criterion.
PMID:29341259 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Multiple exons deletions or microdeletion was not rare in CHILD syndrome. Classical Sanger sequencing may not be useful enough to find all kinds of mutations. Next-generation sequencing may be more effective."
States the assay blind spot directly, and the same report supplies the worked example, a large deletion of NSDHL exons 3 and 4.
PMID:41625319 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The identified structural variants consisted of deletions of varying sizes in the Xq28 cytoband, encompassing regions that contain exons."
The abstract reports structural variants in a mixed incontinentia pigmenti/CHILD cohort. It does not assign every deletion to NSDHL or establish a CHILD-specific diagnostic yield.
+ 1 more reference
Sterol profiling of skin flakes and plasma
Sterol profiling of affected skin can support biochemical interpretation of an NSDHL variant. In one p.Gly44Ser case, skin-flake methyl-, carboxy- and keto-sterol abnormalities supported reclassification from uncertain significance to likely pathogenic. An abnormal methylsterol was also detected in plasma in that patient. Serum profiles can be near normal, so a normal systemic result does not exclude the disorder.
Diagnostic Procedure NCIT:C18020 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:33139364 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Sterol analysis from skin flakes revealed increased levels of a mono 4-alpha methyl sterol also seen in plasma as well as the presence of 4-alpha-carboxy-4-methyl-cholest-8(9)-en-3beta-ol and several keto-sterols, which are usually below the limit of detection. This sterol pattern is consistent..."
Reports the sterol pattern and its interpretation as evidence of the enzymatic block.
PMID:33139364 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"On this basis, this variant has been reclassified as likely pathogenic."
Documents the concrete diagnostic use of the sterol result, resolving a variant of uncertain significance.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK51754/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK51754.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Serum concentrations of methylsterol and cholesterol are almost always normal in individuals with CHILD syndrome and CK syndrome."
GeneReviews limits the usefulness of normal serum sterols for excluding these disorders.
📈

Progression

2
Postnatal improvement of cutaneous lesions
Cutaneous lesions can improve with age, but intertriginous lesions may persist and new lesions can arise later, including at sites of injury.
Show evidence (2 references)
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK51754/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK51754.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Most of the skin lesions improve spontaneously, but some can cause lifelong morbidity. Lesions in the intertriginous areas tend to be more persistent; this is termed ptychotropism"
GeneReviews describes the human cutaneous course.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK51754/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK51754.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Ichthyosiform skin lesions can develop after infancy at sites of injury such as a surgical wound."
Lesions can also appear after early development.
Survival into adulthood
Life span in females is not well characterized. Adults with a wide range of disease severity have been reported, including an individual diagnosed at age 82; that observation does not establish a normal population life expectancy. Pulmonary hypoplasia can cause early respiratory compromise and death.
Show evidence (2 references)
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK51754/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK51754.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"It is unknown whether life span in females with CHILD syndrome is abnormal."
GeneReviews explicitly states uncertainty about life expectancy.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK51754/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK51754.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"CHILD syndrome was reported with phenotypes ranging from mild to severe in three different generations, with the oldest reported individual clinically and molecularly diagnosed at age 82 years"
The source documents survival to old age in a family, not a population survival estimate.

Differential Diagnoses

3

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from CHILD Syndrome:

X-linked chondrodysplasia punctata 2 (Conradi-Hunermann-Happle syndrome) MONDO:0020603
Overlapping Features EBP-related X-linked chondrodysplasia punctata 2 is a distinct sterol-biosynthesis disorder with overlapping ichthyotic skin and skeletal findings. An early patient described clinically as CHILD syndrome had an EBP nonsense variant and a CDPX2 sterol profile. Current GeneReviews places EBP-related CDPX2 in the differential of NSDHL-related CHILD syndrome; the historical label does not establish EBP as a second causative gene for this entry.
Distinguishing Features
  • CDPX2 commonly has bilateral asymmetric skin and skeletal involvement. CHILD often has sharper lateralization, but bilateral CHILD presentations mean distribution alone is not definitive.
  • CDPX2 is caused by EBP variants affecting sterol delta8-delta7 isomerization, a different enzymatic step from NSDHL-dependent C4-demethylation.
  • CDPX2 carries a plasma sterol signature of raised 8-dehydrocholesterol and 8(9)-cholestenol that NSDHL-related CHILD syndrome does not
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:10710235 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"EBP was unaffected in the patients analyzed by us demonstrating that CHILD syndrome and CDPX2 are not caused by allelic mutations."
Establishes that in this cohort the two are genetically distinct despite their shared pathway and overlapping clinical picture.
PMID:10710233 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"although the skeletal defects and skin lesions in CDPX2 are bilateral and asymmetric"
States the clinical distinction in lesion distribution that separates the two on examination.
Inflammatory linear verrucous epidermal nevus (ILVEN) Not Yet Curated MONDO:0019318
Overlapping Features ILVEN can resemble the inflammatory verrucous CHILD nevus. A reported boy initially suspected to have CHILD syndrome was diagnosed with severe ILVEN despite ipsilateral contracture and short stature. Distribution and extracutaneous findings therefore require interpretation together with molecular and biochemical testing.
Distinguishing Features
  • A pathogenic NSDHL variant and the corresponding sterol-biosynthesis abnormality support CHILD syndrome; negative or incomplete testing alone does not prove ILVEN.
  • Both ILVEN and CHILD lesions can follow Blaschko lines, so that pattern alone does not distinguish them.
  • ILVEN lacks the ipsilateral skeletal hypoplasia or aplasia of CHILD syndrome, though an ipsilateral limb contracture has been reported in ILVEN, so limb involvement alone does not settle it
  • CHILD syndrome predominantly affects females, but rare mosaic males are reported; male sex alone does not exclude it.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:39953436 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Initially, we suspected this may be a rare case of CHILD syndrome in a boy. However, after further investigation, we confirmed the diagnosis as a severe form of ILVEN."
A worked case of the differential actually being made, with CHILD syndrome as the initial working diagnosis and ILVEN as the resolution.
PMID:39953436 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"He also had an associated contracture in the ipsilateral limb and short stature."
Documents that the ILVEN case carried ipsilateral limb findings too, which is why extracutaneous involvement does not by itself distinguish the two.
CK syndrome
Overlapping Features CK syndrome is an allelic NSDHL disorder associated with hypomorphic variants. Affected males typically have intellectual disability, behavioral problems, infantile seizures, cortical malformations and microcephaly. This differs from the predominant cutaneous and limb phenotype of CHILD syndrome, although both disorders can have CNS manifestations.
Distinguishing Features
  • CK syndrome typically affects males with hypomorphic NSDHL variants; CHILD syndrome predominantly affects heterozygous females, with rare mosaic males.
  • CK syndrome is dominated by cognitive impairment, seizures, and cortical malformation, whereas intellect is usually normal in CHILD syndrome
  • CK syndrome lacks the lateralized ichthyosiform nevus and ipsilateral limb defects
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21290788 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The diagnosis of CK syndrome is established in a male proband with a hemizygous NSDHL hypomorphic pathogenic variant identified by molecular genetic testing that results in partial loss of functional decarboxylating sterol-4-alpha-carboxylate 3-dehydrogenase."
States the allelic relationship and the partial-loss-of-function basis that separates CK syndrome from CHILD syndrome.
🧫

Experimental Models

4
Heterologous expression of NSDHL missense mutants CELL_LINE
Heterologous expression assays examined thirteen disease-associated NSDHL missense variants and one deletion. Protein abundance was reduced for all tested variants and partly increased for selected variants at lower temperature or with glycerol. The abstract also implicates heat shock proteins in optimal expression, potentially through protein synthesis. These findings do not distinguish instability from impaired synthesis for every variant or demonstrate restored enzyme activity after abundance rescue.
Publication
PMID:40222685
Pathograph links
NSDHL Loss of Function measures fidelity: moderate observes: molecular Measures variant-associated reductions in NSDHL protein abundance and partial abundance rescue in selected conditions.
Limitations: Heterologous overexpression in cultured cells does not report on endogenous expression in keratinocytes or on the mosaic tissue context, and a temperature or glycerol rescue in culture is not evidence that any clinically usable chaperone therapy exists.
Readouts
NSDHL mutant protein expression level
Direction: DECREASED
Interpretation: Steady-state abundance is lower for the tested variants; the assay does not by itself distinguish reduced synthesis from faster degradation.

"Here we show that protein expression levels were low for all mutants, but some could be rescued by a lower temperature (30°C vs. 37°C) and/or the chemical chaperone glycerol."
Reports the expression measurement behind this readout.

"Here we show that protein expression levels were low for all mutants, but some could be rescued by a lower temperature (30°C vs. 37°C) and/or the chemical chaperone glycerol."
The measured endpoint is protein abundance; neither catalytic rescue nor clinical benefit follows from it.

NSDHL-depleted cell models of receptor trafficking CELL_LINE
Experimental NSDHL depletion in carcinoma cell systems alters EGFR endosomal handling. Nsdhl-deficient immortalized mouse embryonic fibroblasts separately show accelerated ligand-induced PDGFR loss. These systems probe receptor trafficking rather than reproducing the full CHILD phenotype.
Publication
PMID:23125191
Pathograph links
Increased Receptor Lysosomal Trafficking perturbs fidelity: moderate observes: cellular NSDHL depletion shifts receptor-associated signals away from recycling endosomes and toward late endosomal compartments.
Limitations: Cancer-cell knockdown and immortalized mouse fibroblasts differ from patient epidermis; results involving SC4MOL alone are not assigned to NSDHL.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:23125191 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"In contrast, depletion of either SC4MOL or NSDHL significantly reduced the amount of fluorescently labeled EGFR ... associated with RAB11-positive recycling endosomes after 30 minutes of EGF stimulation."
NSDHL knockdown in experimental cells reduced receptor localization to recycling endosomes; this is not a patient-tissue result.

🐁

Animal Models

3
Bare patches and striated mice (Nsdhl mutant)
Bare patches and striated Nsdhl mice reproduce an X-linked sterol-biosynthesis defect and mosaic skin involvement. Several alleles cause prenatal male death at E10.5-E13.5, while the severe Bpa1H allele often causes earlier death. Age-related selection is measured in liver and brain; skin, placental, signaling, and hair findings require their own tissue and allele context. Patches and stripes do not reproduce the characteristic human body-half distribution.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
Nsdhl mutant alleles Bpa and Str, X-linked dominant male-lethal
Genes
NSDHL hgnc:13398 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this experimental model concerns this gene This experimental model concerns NSDHL (hgnc:13398). hgnc:13398 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Publication
PMID:10369263
Pathograph links
NSDHL Loss of Function recapitulates fidelity: high observes: molecular Independent Bpa and Str alleles are mutations in Nsdhl, and tissue sterol analysis confirms the biosynthetic role.
Limitations: The mouse skin phenotype is patterned as patches and stripes rather than the lateralized, midline-demarcated distribution seen in humans, so the model is informative for the enzymatic lesion but not for the lateralization.

Conditional Nsdhl ablation in radial glia
Exon-5 deletion driven by hGFAP-cre in radial-glial lineages begins during embryonic CNS development. Mutant males are live-born but develop ataxia and severe postnatal neurodegeneration; the reported animals died at P12-P20. The model establishes consequences of tissue-specific NSDHL loss, not the clinical phenotype of a particular human CK hypomorphic allele. Isolated granule-cell rescue experiments are recorded separately as cell-culture models.

Labrador Retriever NSDHL deletion
A female Labrador Retriever and her crossbred daughter carried a 14,399-base-pair deletion removing the final three NSDHL exons and had a patterned cornification disorder. Their lesions were bilateral and Blaschko-linear, without the typical human limb-reduction phenotype. Two male littermates died at birth but were not genotyped; those deaths cannot establish segregation of a lethal NSDHL genotype.

"Variants in the NSDHL gene cause CHILD syndrome in humans, and the bare patches (Bpa) and striated (Str) phenotypes in mice."
The authors situate their canine finding against the human disease and the established mouse models, which is what makes this dog a model for CHILD syndrome rather than an isolated veterinary case.

click to show 
name: CHILD Syndrome
creation_date: "2026-08-13T19:20:00Z"
category: Mendelian
description: >-
  CHILD syndrome (congenital hemidysplasia with ichthyosiform nevus and limb defects) is an X-linked, usually male-lethal disorder caused by pathogenic NSDHL variants that impair post-squalene cholesterol biosynthesis. Heterozygous females have variable cutaneous and skeletal involvement, typically with unilateral ichthyosiform lesions and ipsilateral limb defects; bilateral or mild presentations and rare mosaic males are reported. Intellect is usually normal. NSDHL participates in sterol C4-demethylation, and experimental models distinguish effects of reduced cholesterol availability from effects of accumulated intermediates. Patient skin shows abnormal epidermal lipid secretion. X-inactivation creates mosaic expression, but the mechanism of the characteristic body-half distribution and any contribution of longitudinal clone depletion in human skin remain unresolved. Topical statins, alone or combined with cholesterol, have improved skin lesions in uncontrolled reports; these observations do not prove that cholesterol supplementation is required or that congenital skeletal defects are corrected. Hypomorphic NSDHL variants cause the distinct allelic disorder CK syndrome.
synonyms:
- CHILD syndrome
- congenital hemidysplasia with ichthyosiform nevus and limb defects
- congenital hemidysplasia with ichthyosiform erythroderma and limb defects
- CHILD nevus
disease_term:
  preferred_term: CHILD syndrome
  term:
    id: MONDO:0010621
    label: CHILD syndrome
classifications:
  harrisons_chapter:
  - classification_value: GENETICS_ENVIRONMENT_DISEASE
    notes: >-
      An X-linked Mendelian disorder whose clinical logic - mosaic expression
      under random X inactivation, male lethality - is genetic rather than
      organ-system based.
  - classification_value: DERMATOLOGY
    notes: >-
      Unilateral ichthyosiform skin lesions are a major presenting manifestation, and topical treatments address the cutaneous component of the disorder.
  - classification_value: ENDOCRINOLOGY_METABOLISM
    notes: >-
      Also an inborn error of metabolism: an enzymatic block in post-squalene
      cholesterol biosynthesis, confirmed by the sterol profile.
  icimd_category:
  - classification_value: sterol_metabolism
    notes: >-
      ICIMD (Ferreira et al. 2021, PMID:33340416): group "Disorders of sterol
      metabolism" under category "Disorders of lipid metabolism". NSDHL is the
      C4-demethylation-complex sterol dehydrogenase/decarboxylase of the distal
      cholesterol pathway.
  isds_skeletal_category:
  - classification_value: chondrodysplasia_punctata
    notes: >-
      ISDS Nosology of Genetic Skeletal Disorders, 2023 revision (Unger et al.,
      PMID:36779427), group 23 "Chondrodysplasia punctata (CDP) group", row
      NOS 23-0030 "Congenital hemidysplasia, ichthyosis, limb defects (CHILD)
      syndrome, NSDHL-related" (MIM 308050). The placement is the nosology's,
      not an inference from the skeletal phenotype: CHILD syndrome is grouped
      with the other CDPs because affected infants show punctate epiphyseal
      calcification, and it sits alongside the other post-squalene
      cholesterol-pathway member of the group, CDPX2 (EBP). Note the group is
      radiographically rather than mechanistically defined, so CHILD's nearest
      mechanistic relatives in dismech - Smith-Lemli-Opitz syndrome and the other
      sterol-biosynthesis disorders - are not group-23 entities.
parents:
- sterol biosynthesis disorder
- X-linked ichthyosis syndrome

inheritance:
- name: X-linked dominant inheritance with male lethality
  inheritance_term:
    preferred_term: X-linked dominant inheritance
    term:
      id: HP:0001423
      label: X-linked dominant inheritance
  description: >-
    CHILD syndrome is inherited in an X-linked manner and predominantly affects heterozygous females because hemizygous males with severe loss-of-function variants usually do not survive gestation. Rare surviving males with postzygotic mosaicism have been reported. A heterozygous mother has a 50% chance of transmitting the pathogenic variant in each pregnancy; the expected live-born distribution is altered by male lethality. Molecular recurrence-risk assessment requires a pathogenic or likely pathogenic variant, not a variant of uncertain significance.

      located at Xq28
    explanation: >-

- name: Functional mosaicism from random X inactivation
  description: >-
    Heterozygous females are functional mosaics because of X-inactivation; this is distinct from a postzygotic DNA variant. In cultures from one studied patient, both NSDHL alleles were present in genomic DNA from affected and unaffected skin. Affected keratinocytes expressed the mutant allele, whereas unaffected keratinocytes expressed the wild-type allele. Affected fibroblasts retained expression of both alleles. This tissue-specific expression pattern does not establish the developmental origin of the body-half boundary or longitudinal clearance of mutant cells in human skin.

genetic:
- name: NSDHL
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: NSDHL
    term:
      id: hgnc:13398
      label: NSDHL
  relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
  notes: >-
    NSDHL loss-of-function variants cause CHILD syndrome. The Xq28 gene encodes the dehydrogenase/decarboxylase component of sterol C4-demethylation. Reported alleles include missense, premature termination, frameshift and exon-deletion variants. Familial transmission can occur with marked variability, including a mother with mild skin changes. Rare male survival can reflect postzygotic mosaicism. Hypomorphic variants retaining partial function cause the distinct allelic CK syndrome.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:10710235
    reference_title: "Mutations in the NSDHL gene, encoding a 3beta-hydroxysteroid dehydrogenase, cause CHILD syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      SSCA and genomic sequence analysis of NSDHL identified in 6 patients with
      CHILD syndrome, including one boy as well as a mother and her daughter,
      mutations potentially impairing protein function.
    explanation: >-
      Establishes NSDHL variants as the molecular cause in a patient series.
  - reference: PMID:21290788
    reference_title: "NSDHL-Related Disorders."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      The diagnosis of CHILD syndrome is established in a female proband with a
      heterozygous NSDHL pathogenic variant identified by molecular genetic
      testing that results in loss of functional decarboxylating
      sterol-4-alpha-carboxylate 3-dehydrogenase, the protein encoded by NSDHL.
    explanation: >-
      GeneReviews states the diagnostic criterion and the loss-of-function
      consequence at the protein level.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
  - reference: PMID:21129721
    reference_title: Hypomorphic temperature-sensitive alleles of NSDHL cause CK syndrome.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: These two mutations, which alter protein folding, show temperature-sensitive protein stability and complementation in Erg26-deficient yeast.
    explanation: The original CK syndrome study demonstrates residual, temperature-sensitive function of its two studied alleles, supporting the distinction from severe CHILD loss of function.
  - reference: PMID:34957706
    reference_title: A novel NSDHL variant in CHILD syndrome with gastrointestinal manifestations and localized skin involvement.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: The variant was confirmed by targeted Sanger sequencing for DNA from both skin tissues and blood. Testing of parental DNA indicated maternal inheritance, with her father testing negative for the variant.
    explanation: The adult gastrointestinal case confirms maternal transmission of the reported NSDHL frameshift.
pathophysiology:
- name: NSDHL Loss of Function
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >-
    Pathogenic NSDHL variants impair the sterol dehydrogenase/decarboxylase component of the C4-demethylation complex. An expression study of thirteen disease-associated missense variants and one deletion found reduced protein abundance; temperature or glycerol partially increased abundance for some variants. These assays do not establish that all variants act through the same folding defect or that rescued protein has restored catalytic activity.
  molecular_functions:
  - preferred_term: sterol-4-alpha-carboxylate 3-dehydrogenase (NSDHL) activity
    modifier: LOSS_OF_FUNCTION
    term:
      id: GO:0000252
      label: 3-beta-hydroxysteroid dehydrogenase [NAD(P)+]/C4-decarboxylase activity

  description: Impaired NSDHL activity limits removal of C4 methyl groups from sterol intermediates. Reduced downstream cholesterol supply and upstream precursor accumulation are distinct biochemical consequences.

- name: Reduced Cholesterol Availability
  description: Cholesterol supply can be insufficient within affected cells despite near-normal circulating sterols. In conditional-null mouse granule-cell cultures, cholesterol or LDL supplementation restored much of the impaired SHH response. This does not imply uniform systemic hypocholesterolemia.

      directness: DIRECT
      snippet: The mutant follicles were smaller and shorter than adjacent WT follicles and lacked hair shafts, consistent with defective anagen hair growth.
      explanation: Mouse mutant skin shows defective postnatal anagen growth; initial P2 follicle morphology was not grossly abnormal.
    - reference: PMID:21753784
      reference_title: Pathogenesis-based therapy reverses cutaneous abnormalities in an inherited disorder of distal cholesterol metabolism.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      directness: DIRECT
      snippet: By 6 months, treated skin largely normalized, but did not grow hair
      explanation: Skin improvement and hair growth were distinct outcomes in two treated patients.

histopathology:
- name: Psoriasiform hyperkeratosis and acanthosis with loss of the granular layer
  description: >-
    Lesional skin shows marked hyperkeratosis and acanthosis with absence of the
    granular layer, together with foam cells in the dermis. This is the routine
    light-microscopic picture, and it reverses on effective pathogenesis-based
    therapy: after three months of topical cholesterol plus lovastatin the
    epidermis was largely normal with a prominent granular layer and
    orthokeratotic scale.

- name: Abnormal lamellar granules on electron microscopy
  description: >-
    Ultrastructurally the lesion is a lipid secretory failure. Vesicular
    structures appear in the intercellular spaces of the stratum corneum and
    vacuoles in the upper prickle cell layer, some recognizable as abnormal
    lamellar granules, with large vacuoles inside the foamy cells of the
    papillary dermis. This is the electron-microscopic counterpart of the
    lamellar body secretory defect modelled in the pathophysiology, observed in
    a separate patient and laboratory from the treatment study.

- phase: Survival into adulthood
  notes: Life span in females is not well characterized. Adults with a wide range of disease severity have been reported, including an individual diagnosed at age 82; that observation does not establish a normal population life expectancy. Pulmonary hypoplasia can cause early respiratory compromise and death.

biochemical:
- name: Methylsterol accumulation in affected tissue
  presence: INCREASED
  notes: >-
    C4-methylsterol intermediates can accumulate in affected tissue. Plasma profiles can be near normal and therefore cannot exclude NSDHL-related disease. A reported p.Gly44Ser case had a characteristic skin-flake sterol profile with an abnormal sterol also detectable in plasma. EBP-related CDPX2 has a different sterol signature and is a differential diagnosis.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:24060582
    reference_title: "The role of abnormalities in the distal pathway of cholesterol synthesis in the Congenital Hemidysplasia with Ichthyosiform erythroderma and Limb Defects (CHILD) syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT

      which leads to inhibition of cholesterol synthesis and accumulation of toxic
      metabolic intermediates in affected tissues
    explanation: >-
      States the accumulation of intermediates and localizes it to affected
      tissues rather than the circulation.

diagnosis:
- name: NSDHL molecular genetic testing
  description: >-
    Diagnosis is supported by characteristic findings and a pathogenic or likely pathogenic NSDHL variant. Sequence analysis detects many coding and splice variants, while exon-level deletion/duplication analysis is needed for copy-number variants that conventional sequencing can miss. A variant of uncertain significance neither establishes nor excludes the diagnosis. Rare mosaic males and mild females require clinical and molecular interpretation rather than exclusion by sex or by absence of a severe limb defect. Optical genome mapping has been reported in a mixed cohort of incontinentia pigmenti and CHILD syndrome; this does not establish a general diagnostic yield for CHILD syndrome.
  diagnosis_term:
    preferred_term: Genetic Testing
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15709
      label: Genetic Testing

      heterozygous NSDHL pathogenic variant identified by molecular genetic
      testing that results in loss of functional decarboxylating
      sterol-4-alpha-carboxylate 3-dehydrogenase, the protein encoded by NSDHL.
    explanation: >-
      GeneReviews states the diagnostic criterion.

- name: Sterol profiling of skin flakes and plasma
  description: >-
    Sterol profiling of affected skin can support biochemical interpretation of an NSDHL variant. In one p.Gly44Ser case, skin-flake methyl-, carboxy- and keto-sterol abnormalities supported reclassification from uncertain significance to likely pathogenic. An abnormal methylsterol was also detected in plasma in that patient. Serum profiles can be near normal, so a normal systemic result does not exclude the disorder.

treatments:
- name: Topical Cholesterol plus Statin (Pathogenesis-Based Therapy)
  description: >-
    Topical lovastatin or simvastatin combined with cholesterol has improved cutaneous lesions in uncontrolled reports. In the original two-patient study, 10% cholesterol alone produced no improvement over three months, followed by substantial improvement with 2% lovastatin/2% cholesterol. There was no statin-only arm, so this sequence does not establish that cholesterol supplementation is required. Later reports describe benefit from statin monotherapy. Skin improvement does not establish correction of congenital skeletal defects, and long-term comparative efficacy and safety remain uncertain.
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Pharmacotherapy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15986
      label: Pharmacotherapy
    therapeutic_agent:
    - preferred_term: cholesterol
      term:
        id: CHEBI:16113
        label: cholesterol

      term:
        id: CHEBI:40303
        label: lovastatin
    - preferred_term: simvastatin
      term:
        id: CHEBI:9150
        label: simvastatin
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Reduced Cholesterol Availability
    treatment_effect: RESTORES
    description: The cholesterol component supplies the pathway end product; tissue-specific target engagement was not isolated from the effects of the statin in the clinical report.
    evidence:

      supports: SUPPORT

      directness: INDIRECT
      snippet: On the basis of the putative pathogenic role of both pathway-product deficiency of cholesterol and accumulation of toxic metabolic intermediates, we assessed the efficacy of combined therapy with lovastatin and cholesterol.
      explanation: The authors state the dual treatment rationale as putative. The combined intervention does not isolate restoration of cholesterol from suppression of precursor formation.
  - target: C4-Methylsterol Accumulation
    treatment_effect: INHIBITS

- name: Topical Simvastatin Monotherapy
  description: Uncontrolled reports describe improvement with topical simvastatin without added cholesterol. In an infant treated with 2% ointment twice daily, improvement appeared by three weeks and the skin largely normalized by six weeks, with maintenance treatment over ten months. A later 5% report describes variable responses by site and recurrent lesions; it follows the previously reported p.Gly44Ser patient rather than adding an independent genotype case. Comparative efficacy and long-term safety are not established.

- name: Topical Ketoconazole
  description: Off-label topical 2% ketoconazole has improved cutaneous lesions in individual reports. In one adult using it twice daily, itch improved after two days, erythema and scaling improved by day seven, and lesions were substantially improved by day 40. DLQI decreased from 3 to 1, with no apparent adverse effects during two and a half months of follow-up. This uncontrolled observation does not establish equivalence to statins or durable remission after stopping treatment.

- name: Genetic Counseling

- name: Bare patches and striated mice (Nsdhl mutant)
  species: Mouse
  genotype: Nsdhl mutant alleles Bpa and Str, X-linked dominant male-lethal
  publication: PMID:10369263
  description: >-
    Bare patches and striated Nsdhl mice reproduce an X-linked sterol-biosynthesis defect and mosaic skin involvement. Several alleles cause prenatal male death at E10.5-E13.5, while the severe Bpa1H allele often causes earlier death. Age-related selection is measured in liver and brain; skin, placental, signaling, and hair findings require their own tissue and allele context. Patches and stripes do not reproduce the characteristic human body-half distribution.
  genes:
  - preferred_term: NSDHL
    term:
      id: hgnc:13398
      label: NSDHL

    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      They provide animal models for the study of CHILD syndrome, a further human
      condition due to mutations in a gene of the cholesterol synthesis pathway.
    explanation: >-
      The human gene-discovery paper explicitly nominates these mouse mutants as
      models for CHILD syndrome, which is the model-level claim.

    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: HIGH
    description: >-

      analysis confirms the biosynthetic role.
    limitations: >-
      The mouse skin phenotype is patterned as patches and stripes rather than the
      lateralized, midline-demarcated distribution seen in humans, so the model is
      informative for the enzymatic lesion but not for the lateralization.

experimental_models:
- name: Heterologous expression of NSDHL missense mutants
  experimental_model_type: CELL_LINE
  publication: PMID:40222685
  description: >-
    Heterologous expression assays examined thirteen disease-associated NSDHL missense variants and one deletion. Protein abundance was reduced for all tested variants and partly increased for selected variants at lower temperature or with glycerol. The abstract also implicates heat shock proteins in optimal expression, potentially through protein synthesis. These findings do not distinguish instability from impaired synthesis for every variant or demonstrate restored enzyme activity after abundance rescue.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:40222685
    reference_title: "Comprehensive survey of disease-causing missense mutations of the cholesterol synthesis enzyme NSDHL: Low temperature and a chemical chaperone rescue low protein expression of select mutants."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: >-
      We examined 13 missense mutations and one deletion mutation in the
      cholesterol synthesis enzyme NSDHL (NAD(P) Dependent Steroid
      Dehydrogenase-Like), known to cause the X-linked developmental disorders
      CHILD (congenital hemidysplasia with ichthyosiform erythroderma and limb
      defects) syndrome and CK syndrome.
    explanation: >-
      States that the alleles assayed in this system are the ones that cause CHILD
      syndrome, which is the model-level relevance claim.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: NSDHL Loss of Function
    relationship: MEASURES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    description: >-
      Measures variant-associated reductions in NSDHL protein abundance and partial abundance rescue in selected conditions.
    limitations: >-
      Heterologous overexpression in cultured cells does not report on endogenous
      expression in keratinocytes or on the mosaic tissue context, and a
      temperature or glycerol rescue in culture is not evidence that any
      clinically usable chaperone therapy exists.
    readouts:
    - name: NSDHL mutant protein expression level
